#### THE SETTLED DESIGN, PER ITEM (the readings the brief left to the implementer)
1. **Posture DERIVED.** `Posture.derive(legacyMode:vaultHasAppleHeldCopy:keyFile:)` — a pure function, in this precedence: (a) the pre-2a stored mode READS THROUGH until §11 retires it (a 13p Castle store stays Castle on the marker-8 build's first launch — `.biometric`/`.passphrase` ⇒ Castle, `.auto` ⇒ Standard); (b) the vault's word when enrolled — an Apple-held copy of the master exists ⇒ Standard, none ⇒ Castle (decision 8: every device follows the account); (c) local-only: the key file's `apple` copy ⇒ Standard, else a passphrase wrap ⇒ Castle; (d) NO SECRETS AT ALL (no vault, no key file, no wraps) ⇒ Standard — the safety arm, because a library nobody can re-open by any word must not demand one. `StoreAtOpenMode.effective(posture:dials:)`: Standard ⇒ `.auto`; Castle ⇒ `.biometric`, or `.passphrase` under Always ask. The legacy read (`ProtectionDefaults.legacyMode`) is per store id first, then the scope's — and returns nil once the store has written its OWN `alwaysAsk`, so a NEW library minted over an absent store never inherits the scope's old Castle. `ProtectionDials` = Castle's two switches only (`alwaysAsk`, `relockAfterIdleMinutes` default 10; choices 5 · 10 · 30 · never); the idle clock is time since Variorum was last used (`IdleLockClock.isDue`, fed by an app-local `NSEvent` monitor on the Mac and the phone's relock coordinator). Lock Now runs the composition root's `lock()`; after the pool closes, `StoreBackup.performLockTimeCopyIfDue` byte-copies the encrypted file (no WAL pending, a backup due, the app's own folder only) with NO key leg, records the copy's name in a pending list, and the next keyed run's `checkPendingLockTimeCopies` integrity-checks it and writes the line into that run's detail. A gate-style change is ONE transition moving BOTH custody items — `ProtectionController.moveCustody(to:)`: the store key via `CustodyHandover` first, then the sync record via `SyncCustodyTransition.move`; a refused sync half REVERSES the key's move, so the two families move together or not at all.
2. **A's item family.** `AppleKeyKeychain` is the ONE query builder — `kSecAttrSynchronizable = true`, the shared group `$(AppIdentifierPrefix)io.lduarte.Variorum.shared`, the data-protection marker, `AfterFirstUnlock`; the service `io.lduarte.Variorum.apple-key`, the account `<scope>-apple-key` (the dev scope has its own A by construction — the spike's surprise 4). `readOrMint` is idempotent; production NEVER deletes (the flagless delete is a no-op at the query level; only the test-flagged delete releases — pinned in the round trip). The reconcile's Apple arm (Standard, key in hand): read A or mint it, land the key file's `apple` copy under `appleKeyID`, VERIFY it opens the key (never adopt), and re-land a copy that sits under another A; under Castle the arm never touches A and reads `appleKeyVerified = false`. A refused A write reads as not-landed, never covered. The escrow family and both DEBUG probe doors, the probe brain and their tests are DELETED (row 12); the legacy escrow ITEMS are removed at the migration under the R1-m1 ownership guard (only when the escrowed key IS this store's key).
3. **Standard's open road (§3.8).** The route over an encrypted Standard store: the bound item opens silently; a MISSING bound item tries A against the key file's `apple` copy (`StoreLobby.openWithAppleKey` — `.opened` re-lands the bound item, R1-M3's rule); `.noAppleCopy` / `.appleKeyAbsent` / `.differentAppleID` become `AppBootstrap.State.waitingForICloudKeychain(StoreLobby)` — a real state, copy §2d (the different-Apple-ID sentence when the key file names another A), whose `Enter Passphrase…` / `Enter Twelve Words…` doors run the ordinary envelope road, and whose Apple road is re-probed every 5 s (`beginWaiting(every:)` — no notification exists for a synchronizable item's arrival; the lookup's fact) until A lands, then opens and re-lands. Never a silent mint over an existing store; nothing lands in custody before a secret or A opens. The probe/nudge tiers re-based on `appleKeyVerified`: covered (A verified) · exposed (Standard, A unreadable — the ONLY signal for "iCloud Keychain off", which is undetectable directly) · unknown.
4. **Row 42.** Every snapshot is a PAIR — `variorum-backup-<stamp>-<fp8>.sqlite` + `.keys.json`, the key file copied WITH the snapshot and never rewritten (a later passphrase change leaves the earlier pair byte-identical; the folder's `variorum-keys.json` is written ONLY when absent, for 1a-era snapshots); prune and remove take the pair; the lock-time copy's pair is written the same way.
5. **The §11 migration.** `ProtectionMigration.run(facts:leaves:)` at the FIRST ready of a store id (idempotent — a done leaf per store; a refused leg returns without marking done so the next ready retries), OFF the main thread by construction, awaited before the reconcile. Legs, in order, each logging one line and never key material: (1) the sync custody re-scoped from the 14b items into the per-store pair under the gate the route used — the legacy GATED item's material read under the lobby's ONE evaluated context at unlock (`StoreKeyGuard.unlockGated` → `SyncCustodySession.holdLegacyMaterial`) and taken here, else the plain item; the identity from `sync_state.device_id` (then the row deleted), else minted; the legacy items deleted and the CF-12 opt-in retired only after write+verify; (2) the legacy escrow item removed iff it holds THIS store's key, else left and said; (3) the dial keys retired to the per-store keys (the read-through ends); (4) custody ALIGNED to the derived posture (both items in one transition; no biometry ⇒ Always ask; a refusal leaves the run undone); (5) the key file gains the master copy + the corpus key id from the held material, and the vault's KDF descriptor when the reconcile READ reached the vault (else at the next online ready); (6) for an enrolled library, the one-time passphrase confirm is marked pending — `MigrationConfirmModel` (copy §9 verbatim) verifies the typed word against the VAULT (`enroll` + `fetchVault`), re-wraps the local passphrase copy under the vault's descriptor (`reconcilePassphraseWrap`), clears the mark; a wrong word writes nothing; *Later* hides the face for this launch and keeps the mark. The production leaves are built in `AppBootstrap.init(variant:gate:)` and NEVER in the test host (`KeychainTestTripwire.armed`).

#### ORCHESTRATOR-DECIDED NOTES (settled under the mandate without the author — for the cold reviewer)
- The four binding facts of the BATCH 2 brief: (i) `StoreAtOpenMode` becomes the DERIVED effective gate, the stored three-way setting deleted; (ii) the 13p escrow family put the RAW library key in iCloud Keychain — deleted, and every legacy escrow item REMOVED at the migration under the R1-m1 ownership guard; (iii) the keyed → keyed re-key twin of `StoreEncryptionMigration` (2b's); (iv) `pullToHead` as a named engine primitive (2b's).
- The three-session split of batch 2 under ONE acceptance unit (2a → 2b → 2c), the unit closing at the batch 2 sitting (= the deploy window).
- The same-Mac half of the A road is 2a's evidence; the cross-device fact (Q3) is verified at her sitting — until then the iCloud road is treated as unverified.

#### DRILLS RUN
- **The migration drill (`ProtectionMigrationDrillTests`, opt-in, run once 2026-09-05 in the signed test host — a scratch encrypted store dressed in the 13p-era shape under a THROWAWAY scope: the 14b plain E2EE item under a throwaway service, the legacy-scope bound store-key item, the legacy synchronizable escrow item holding the RAW key, a `sync_state.device_id` row, the 13p dial keys; the vault's word scripted as Castle):**
```
dress: escrow add 0 (No error.)
BEFORE inventory:
  PRESENT — e2ee legacy plain (device-keys, throwaway service)
  absent  — e2ee per-store GATED (-sync-biometric)
  absent  — e2ee per-store bound (-sync)
  PRESENT — legacy ESCROW (synchronizable, raw key)
  PRESENT — store-key legacy bound (drill-abfb1120-bound)
  absent  — store-key per-store GATED (biometric)
  absent  — store-key per-store bound
BEFORE sync_state.device_id = drill-device-13j · dial keys: mode=auto
route-time gate (the legacy mode reads through): auto · posture standard · biometry available: true
migration 2a: begins for store 4cfa62a3… under auto
migration 2a: sync custody re-scoped → done (identity from sync_state)
migration 2a: legacy iCloud escrow item removed → true
migration 2a: dial keys retired to the per-store keys
migration 2a: custody aligned auto → biometric: done
migration 2a: key file gains the master copy + corpus key id + the vault's descriptor
migration 2a: passphrase confirm pending (the one-time face)
migration 2a: done
AFTER inventory:
  absent  — e2ee legacy plain (device-keys, throwaway service)
  PRESENT — e2ee per-store GATED (-sync-biometric)
  PRESENT — e2ee per-store bound (-sync)
  absent  — legacy ESCROW (synchronizable, raw key)
  absent  — store-key legacy bound (drill-abfb1120-bound)
  PRESENT — store-key per-store GATED (biometric)
  absent  — store-key per-store bound
AFTER sync_state.device_id = nil · legacy dial keys: retired · alwaysAsk=false idle=Optional(15) · opt-in key: retired · confirm pending: true
```
  Read: leg 1 re-scoped the sync custody from the legacy plain item into the per-store pair under the SILENT shape the route used (the identity taken from `sync_state`, the row deleted after); leg 2 removed the legacy escrow item because it held THIS store's key; leg 3 retired the scope-wide dial keys into the store's own (the idle 15 carried, `alwaysAsk` false); leg 4 aligned custody `auto → biometric` — the store key AND the sync record moved to their gated items in one transition (both `-biometric` items PRESENT after, both bound items absent); leg 5 wrote the key file's master copy + the corpus key id + the vault's descriptor from the held material; leg 6 left the one-time confirm pending. The AFTER inventory shows exactly the per-store custody of the design and NONE of the 13p-era items. Pollution: none — every item under `drill-<uuid>` accounts and the throwaway service, deleted at the end of the test.
- **The A round trip (`AppleKeyRoundTripTests`, opt-in `TEST_RUNNER_VARIORUM_APPLE_KEY_ROUND_TRIP=1`, run once 2026-09-05 — the REAL family under a throwaway account):**
```

flagged delete: released true — gone: true
flagless delete: -25300 (The specified item could not be found in the keychain.) — item still there: true
read: id 465c1764-b772-4f48-a6b5-5b5e6ebc4883 — equal: true
readOrMint (again): equal to the first: true
readOrMint (mint): id 465c1764-b772-4f48-a6b5-5b5e6ebc4883, 32 bytes: true
*[removed for publication]*
```

#### SUITE COUNTS

#### MACHINERY NOTES
- The batch's DerivedData: `App/Variorum/DerivedData/pre13x-b0` (reused); logs beside it as `2a-*.log` (gitignored).
- **THE HANG (2026-09-05, the first launch of the test host with the live migration wired):** `xcodebuild test` reported *the test runner timed out while preparing to run tests* after `[protection] migration 2a: begins for store 4077e8a5… under auto` — the dev store's — and nothing more. `sample` of the host: the MAIN thread parked in `ProtectionMigration.run` → `E2EEKeychainStorage.legacyLoadPlain` → `SecItemCopyMatching` → `SecKeychainItemCopyContent` → securityd decrypt (`mach_msg`) — the login-keychain ACL road. Launching the dev-store Debug APP itself showed the dialog: *"Variorum Dev wants to use your confidential information stored in \"io.lduarte.Variorum.e2ee-keys\" in your keychain. To allow this, enter the \"login\" keychain password."* (screenshot `App/Variorum/DerivedData/2a-devapp-launch.png`, gitignored; `security find-generic-password` without `-w`: the item was created 2026-08-31 07:24 UTC in `login.keychain-db` — a Debug build's, whose signature this rebuilt binary does not match). Two consequences, both fixed here: (1) **the migration ran its Keychain legs on the MainActor** — under approachable concurrency a nonisolated async function runs on its CALLER's actor and the bootstrap calls from the MainActor — so `run` now hops to a detached utility task by construction (the KeychainOffMain law; pinned: `theKeychainLegsNeverRunOnTheMainThread` records `Thread.isMainThread` in the Keychain-class leaves — red without the hop); (2) **the test host must never run the live migration over the dev scope** — the tripwire refuses every real-scope Keychain mutation there, so the road could only ever end `writeRefused` at every launch — `AppBootstrap.init(variant:gate:)` builds no production leaves when `KeychainTestTripwire.armed` (the drill under throwaway scopes is the real-Keychain proof; the bootstrap's own migration road is pinned through injected leaves). What is NOT fixed, deliberately: the dev scope's legacy item stays behind its prompt — answering it needs her login-keychain password, which no session types; the dev store's app-level run is handed forward. **For the WINDOW (her real store):** the Release app has read its own legacy item on every sync round since 14b without a prompt (its ACL trusts the app), so the migration's read is silent there; the one leg the drill cannot show under the real ACL is `SecItemDelete` of the legacy login-keychain items — if a prompt appears at that leg on window day, it is this class, and *Always Allow* is the answer.
- **Suite names are TYPES, and a file is not a type:** `-only-testing:VariorumTests/Phase13pProtectionBrainTests` matched NOTHING (the file holds `ProtectionDialsTests`, `ProtectionDialsPerStoreTests`, `ProtectionCadenceTests`, `ProtectionCopyTests`, `StoreLaunchRouteTests`, `StoreKeyGuardBackingTests`), and xcodebuild exited 0 with `Executed 0 tests` — caught only because the `Test run with N tests` line was missing. The spine file likewise holds TEN types (the standing 72/10 is ten suites); the gate run names all ten.
- **The accidental full Mac run at 2a.1** (an ugrep multi-file string bug in a suite-discovery command ran the whole `VariorumTests` bundle): 2100/378 with ONE failure — `setPassphraseRefusesOverAnUnreadableSidecar…` — a real 2a defect (the reset door read a stale envelope projection), fixed at `41afd430` (`resetPassphrase` refreshes the envelope facts first, the P2 pattern). Recorded because the accident found it.
- **The commit message of `9f28f4d8` says "lobby suites 22/2"; the run was 15/2** (the log). A miscount at the message, not the log; the record's counts are the log's.
- **The batch C off-main pin** (`theKeychainLegsNeverRunOnTheMainThread`) went red under the LEGS revert at `:267` — its "…and the run did run" clause, not its off-main clause (no leg reached a Keychain leaf); its own mechanism was reverted and proven in batch A at `:266`.

#### QUESTIONS FOR THE AUTHOR / ARCHITECT
1. **Copy §9's first sentence is stale against the final design** — *"Variorum now keeps your sync keys inside your encrypted library"* — the exact phrase `e499f5ca` corrected elsewhere (the keys live in per-store custody beside the library key, NEVER in the file). Rendered VERBATIM per the render-don't-re-derive rule and flagged here: the orchestrator should correct the copy file (copy §4's enrolled line has the true sentence: *"This Mac keeps your sync keys in its own protected Keychain, beside your library's key."*) and the string follows in one edit before the window.
2. **A Standard library whose A write was REFUSED that then sets a passphrase derives Castle at its next route** (local-only, no vault): the key file holds a passphrase wrap and no `apple` copy, and rule (c) reads Castle. Rare (the Keychain-refusal class) and self-describing (the lobby says Castle), but it is a posture the user never chose; the honest fix is the reconcile re-trying the Apple landing at every Standard ready (it does — `reconcileAppleKey` runs at each ready; the corner is the ONE route between a refused landing and the next ready). Recorded, not changed — the architect's call whether the route should also try to land A before deriving.
3. **"iCloud Keychain is off" is undetectable on macOS** (the lookup's fact: a synchronizable `SecItemAdd` succeeds with iCloud Keychain off; no toggle API; no arrival notification). The exposed tier therefore fires only when A is UNREADABLE, never when it merely will not sync — a Standard library on a Mac with iCloud Keychain off reads "covered" while its key would not reach a second device. Copy §5's covered line says what the code can know; the sitting may want a sentence for the off case (the phone's arrival is the only proof, Q3).
4. **Copy §9's confirm sheet renders the stale sentence verbatim** (question 1) — the one-time face every enrolled user meets at the window says "inside your encrypted library". Fix the copy file before the window; the face re-renders it unchanged.
5. **The dev store's own §11 run needs her login-keychain password once** (the Debug app's ACL prompt — MACHINERY NOTES); until then the dev store stays in the 13p shape, opening as before. Not a defect; a fact of the dev scope's 2026-08-31 item.

1. **Copy §9's stale sentence (questions 1 and 4) — FIXED in the copy file** (the sync keys live in this device's protected Keychain beside the library key, never in the file); the rendered string + its pin are 2b's item (0).
2. **A refused Apple landing followed by a passphrase set derived Castle (question 2) — RULED (architect), corner 45:** Castle is ENTERED, and entering moves the raw key behind Touch ID or nowhere; so with no `apple` copy the custody SHAPE tells Standard-waiting (the raw key in the silent bound item) from Castle (the gated item or nowhere) by presence alone — arm (c) gains the discriminator and the route tries the landing before deriving; §3.1's posture row carries it; 2b builds it (item 0).
3. **"iCloud Keychain is off" is undetectable (question 3) — ACCEPTED, corner 46:** the covered tier's sentence now hedges in the copy file ("as far as this Mac can tell"); Q3's phone arrival is the only proof.
4. **The dev store's login-keychain prompt (question 5) — HERS, bundled for the sitting** (the checklist §5 carries it, and the real store's possible prompt at the DELETE leg — Always Allow).
5. The `Variorum-dev-apple-key` item in her iCloud Keychain (the dev scope's own A) is left as recorded.

- **2b.6 the drills (the signed host, throwaway scopes; a scratch keyed store seeded with the sample library; a fake-server sibling): Standard → Castle · Castle → Standard · a crash at each of the three seams re-run to completion · the wall time:** DONE (`a2981116`) — `App/Variorum/VariorumTests/PostureSwitchDrillTests.swift`, OPT-IN under `TEST_RUNNER_VARIORUM_POSTURE_DRILL=1` (the 2a precedent), RUN TWICE to green (the first two runs found DRILL-side faults only — the vault emulation held weakly by the client's `load` seam and freed at `makeWorld`'s return; the seeder's `uuid`/`date`/random dependencies unimplemented in the test context; three assertions mis-stated: the real `.passphrase` custody keeps the DEVICE IDENTITY in the plain sync item with `material: nil` (1a's design — the keys leave, the identity stays), a fresh sibling's own three seeded settings arrive as MERGES not applies, and a resume's `Outcome.reencrypted` counts THIS run's rows only — the mirror's batches carry the whole count across a crash). #### THE SETTLED DESIGN, PER ITEM (filled as each lands)
*(the LIVE STATUS lines above carry the per-item detail at commit granularity; this is the shape, item by item, as it stands at the gate)*
- **(0) the seam corrections:** copy §9 rendered corrected (the keys in per-store custody beside the library key, never in the file); `Posture.derive` reads the RAW key's custody PRESENCE (corner 45 — a refused Apple landing never makes Castle; the route tries the landing before deriving).
- **(1) the ONE rotation runner (`PostureSwitchRunner`, Shared) with `entersCastle`:** §4's seven steps in order over injectable leaves; Replace All Keys = the same road with `entersCastle: false` (the Apple-held copy KEPT under Standard — decision 30; custody re-saved silent). `AccountRekey`'s truncate road is GONE; `account_data_truncate` keeps its fence for the headless tool only. The secrets are INPUTS (`RotationSecrets` from the typed passphrase + the words in hand) — question 1.
- **(1b) `pullToHead` + `outboxCount` as engine primitives;** step 1 = ONE `push()` + `pullToHead` + an empty-outbox check (a backlog beyond one push's `maxPushIterations × batchSize` refuses `outboxNotEmpty` with the count — question 2).
- **(1c) the vault write under the OLD proof** (`CorpusRotation.begin`): M′ under P and W, C′ and the previous key + id under M′, the new hash, the seq claimed inside the RPC; the driver (`reencryptRemainder` — N of M through `progress`) SHARED with 2c's follower; `complete` clears key, id and seq together under the CURRENT proof.
- **(1d) the per-STEP marker** (`RotationMarker` in `sync_state.rekey_in_progress`: vaultWrite · reencrypt · rekeyLibrary · custody · snapshot · offer + the fingerprints and the corpus key id, never a key), written BEFORE the vault write, advanced per step, cleared at the end of step 6; `resume` re-runs from the failed step (the vault re-read tells at `.vaultWrite`/`.reencrypt`; the file PROBED at `.rekeyLibrary`).
- **(2) the keyed → keyed re-key** (`StoreEncryptionMigration.rekey`) on the same `Step`s; idempotent by probe; run through the bootstrap's `rekeyLibrary(from:to:work:)` (flush → `.opening` → the pool closed → the work → reopen under whichever key opens the file → re-adopt).
- **(3) the NEW key file FIRST** under the new fingerprint (L′ under P with the vault's fresh descriptor, W, M′; the Apple copy only under Standard's Replace All Keys; the vault's descriptor + corpus key id recorded); the OLD key file stays beside old snapshots.
- **(4) custody through `moveCustody(to:)`:** Touch ID, or Always ask where chosen or no biometry, entering Castle; silent under Standard's Replace All Keys; `.auto` leaving Castle.
- **(5) the fresh snapshot PAIR before the offer; the scheduled `.md` insurance OFF with the notice (entering Castle only); the offer over the app's own PRE-switch pairs (every snapshot whose fingerprint is not the new key's), removed as PAIRS; no folder file ever rewritten.**
- **Leaving Castle:** A read-or-minted; ONE vault write adding M under A under the current proof; the key file's `apple` copy of L; `moveCustody(.auto)`; re-lock off by derivation; NOTHING rotated; no marker.
- **Without sync:** steps 4–7 only (the secrets landing on the key file alone); the resume at `.rekeyLibrary` asks for the passphrase (no master copy).
- **The ceremony (`PostureSwitchModel`, Shared; the Mac sheet):** the door check FIRST (decision 32's sentence with the vault's day; the offline sentence); copy §2b's screens in order — the passphrase set only if none (vault-verified when enrolled), the words typed-or-regenerated when they exist (option (A), PROVISIONAL) or generated + confirmed by exact re-entry, the floor with the passphrase verified against the VAULT's current copy (a retired word refused there) else the key file, During from the runner's phase, Done + the notice, the offer; §2c's leave confirm by Touch ID (`confirmPresence`) or the passphrase; the resume road; the ceremony HELD on the controller across the re-key's reopen. The doors render only when a runner is wired; a pending marker offers its resume instead of a second start.


#### ORCHESTRATOR-DECIDED NOTES (settled under the mandate without the author — for the cold reviewer)
- The rotation runner REPLACES `AccountRekey`'s 14c truncate road — in place through `sync_reencrypt`, never truncating; `account_data_truncate` keeps its fence for the headless `vault-bootstrap` tool alone.
- `pullToHead` + the outbox count are ENGINE primitives (fact iv), never inlined.
- The keyed → keyed re-key is `StoreEncryptionMigration`'s twin on the same `Step`s (fact iii).
- The per-step persisted marker on `AccountRekeyMarker`'s pattern, resuming at the failed step.
- Corner 45's discriminator (a refused Apple landing never makes Castle) and the corrected copy §9 sentence.
- **The migration's log line persists (the orchestrator's addition, mid-batch):** `ProtectionMigration.Leaves.live`'s `log` writes at `notice`, not `info` — macOS does not persist info-level entries, so `log show` could never read the `migration 2a:` lines the window's checklist has the orchestrator read (verified: zero lines in 24 h). Still never key material (the no-key-material pin rides the leaves, level-independent; asserting the level would need an OSLog read — not cheap, not done).
- **The marker clears at the end of step 6, not after the offer (the implementer's reading of "cleared only after step 7"):** the offer is a CHOICE and *Keep Them* is a valid end; a marker that held every sync round hostage until an OFFER was answered — or forever, if the sheet was dismissed — would refuse rounds for no mechanism. The offer's listing rides the outcome in memory.
- **The T1 recency guard retired with the truncate road it guarded:** an in-place rotation clobbers nothing on the server, so "the server holds N notes this copy doesn't have" is no longer a warning the door owes.

- **The drills run as a NO-BIOMETRY machine** (`biometryAvailable` false ⇒ Castle's gate is the passphrase, no Keychain item): a Touch ID item's ACL read would PROMPT inside a test that nothing can answer; the Touch ID target is pinned with in-memory backings, the gated add's landing is `Phase13pKeychainFactTests`' standing proof. The dial `alwaysAsk` stays FALSE in that state — Always ask is Castle-only (`setAlwaysAsk`) and a no-biometry Castle is the passphrase gate by derivation, not by the dial.
- **`ProtectionController.refreshEnvelopeFacts()` made internal** (was private) so the ceremony pins can prime a scripted controller's envelope facts without a reconcile; `closeSwitch()` calls it too.
- **The Backups tab's plaintext warning names Castle** (`plaintextExportUnderLockedDown` — the 13p "locked down" word replaced; its pin's phrase kept) — the copy file's §8 spirit, since the switch turns the export OFF with §2b's notice and a re-enable under Castle is exactly this warning's moment.

#### DRILLS RUN (2b.6 — `App/Variorum/DerivedData/2b6-drill3.log`, the green run; `2b6-drill.log` / `2b6-drill2.log` the two drill-side fault runs)

**Drill 1 — Standard → Castle with a sibling, then Castle → Standard** (`standardToCastleWithASiblingThenBackToStandard`), verbatim from the host's stdout:
```
drill 2b: seeded the sample library in 2.921444959 seconds — 2533 live takes; pushed 7656 rows under the old corpus key in 1.4490435 seconds
drill 2b: snapshot pair written: variorum-backup-2026-09-05-111956-8e79aff7.sqlite
drill 2b: posture switch: the vault rewritten under the old proof — rotation seq 7657
drill 2b: posture switch: re-encrypted 7656 rows; the previous key cleared
drill 2b: posture switch: the new key file written (ebdbaef6)
drill 2b: re-keyed the library file in 0.054684709 seconds (under L′)
drill 2b: snapshot pair written: variorum-backup-2026-09-05-112000-ebdbaef6.sqlite
drill 2b: posture switch: the scheduled plaintext export turned OFF
drill 2b: posture switch: Castle entered — library key ebdbaef6
drill 2b: STANDARD → CASTLE in 4.094904542 seconds — 7656 rows re-encrypted; insurance off: true; pre-switch pairs: ["variorum-backup-2026-09-05-111956-8e79aff7.sqlite"]
drill 2b: the followed sibling pulled: applied 7656, merged 0, cursor 15313
drill 2b: leaving Castle: the vault gained the Apple-held copy under 322dd9ac
drill 2b: leaving Castle: done — custody silent, the Apple copies landed
drill 2b: CASTLE → STANDARD in 0.013491208 seconds
```
Asserted green around those lines: the library under L′ alone (the old L and the Standard-era key file's A-copy open nothing); L′ under P, W and M′, no Apple copy; the real Keychain after the switch — the silent store-key item GONE, no gated item, the plain sync item holding ONLY the device identity (`material: nil`), the session holding M′/C′, the controller's resident L′, `posture == .castle`; the vault's previous key + seq cleared together, no Apple-held copy, the corpus key id = custody's; the marker nil; the sibling on the old keys REFUSED at the fence (`encryptionKeysReplaced`), then FOLLOWED by the passphrase (`VaultEnrollment.enroll` → M′, no rotation left) and pulled clean; a FRESH device under the new keys applied every row (7,653 applied + 3 merged — its own seeded settings — = 7,656) and holds all 2,533 takes with a sampled long take byte-identical; the offer listed exactly the pre-switch pair, whose bytes (snapshot AND pair key file) were untouched; the fresh pair carries the new fp8; Delete Them removed the pair together and the fresh one survived; the insurance OFF. Leaving Castle: the vault's Apple-held copy under A (M recovered by A), the key file's Apple copy of L′, custody SILENT again (the real silent item holds L′; the plain sync record holds C′), `posture == .standard`, the corpus key id unchanged, the mirror untouched.

**Drill 2 — a crash at each of the three seams, re-run to completion over the same corpus** (`aCrashAtEachSeamResumesToCompletionOverTheSampleLibrary`; a fresh world per seam — each seeds + pushes the corpus again):
```
drill 2b: SEAM A (mid re-encryption) resumed to completion in 2.227585375 seconds
drill 2b: SEAM B (before the swap) resumed to completion in 0.683283125 seconds — no passphrase needed (the master copy)
drill 2b: SEAM C (after the swap, before custody) resumed to completion in 0.646537541 seconds
```
Seam A: the mirror's first `reencrypt` threw (a network loss mid-walk) — the marker at `.reencrypt`, custody carrying the previous key, the silent item still L; `resume(passphrase:recoveryWords:)` finished the walk (the mirror's batches total 7,656 across the crash) and steps 4–6. Seam B: the re-key leaf threw before the export — the marker at `.rekeyLibrary`, the file under L; `resume(passphrase: nil)` opened L′ from the new key file's master copy, redid the swap, moved custody, took the pair. Seam C: the leaf threw AFTER the export-and-swap — the file already under L′, the silent item still L; the resume never exported again (`rekeys` unchanged), moved custody, took the pair. Every seam ends in the same end state as drill 1 (the same `expectCastleEndState`).

**The wall time, for the sitting's real-store estimate:** 7,656 rows / 2,533 takes — the vault write + the walk + completion ≈ 3.5 s against an IN-MEMORY mirror (the re-encryption is CPU-bound AES-GCM open/seal per row: 39 batches of 200), the library re-key (export-and-swap of the whole Sherlock corpus under SQLCipher) **0.055 s**, custody + the snapshot pair ≈ 0.5 s; **Standard → Castle 4.1 s end to end; Castle → Standard 0.013 s.** Against the real mirror the walk's time is the network's (39 round trips of `sync_pull` + `sync_reencrypt`, each ~200 rows) — the sitting's real-store measurement is the honest number; the ceremony's During line shows N of M.

#### HANDED FORWARD
- **To 2c (the follow + the phone):** the SHARED driver's follower entry is `CorpusRotation.materialFollowing(_:master:)` (C′ + the previous key + seq under M′, pinned in `CorpusRotationTests`) + `reencryptRemainder(material:)` — "any device holding M′ can finish step 3" is ONE call; the ceremony's ENTIRE brain is `PostureSwitchModel` (Shared) — the phone renders its phases (the Mac's `PostureSwitchSheet` is the reference face); the phone's `rekeyRunner` wiring (`EncryptionSession.rekeyRunner`) is NOT wired on the phone — `AppServices.runAccountRekey` is gone, the ROOT wires the runner (`VariorumApp.swift`'s `.ready` task builds `PostureSwitchRunner(leaves: .live(...))` and sets `bootstrap.protection.switchRunner` + `encryption.rekeyRunner`); the phone needs the same two lines over its own bootstrap/services; the "still finishing" sentence's device is "another device" (the vault carries no device name — question 4); the notices (`insuranceTurnedOffNotice`, `resumeBody`) are copy-file strings, ready.
- **To batch 3 (Replace All Keys' door + the checkboxes):** the runner serves it byte-for-byte — `PostureSwitchRunner.replaceAllKeys(passphrase:recoveryWords:)` = the same road with `entersCastle: false` (the Apple-held copy kept, custody re-saved silent, no insurance change, no offer); `EncryptionSession.rekeyAccount` already calls it (the Encryption tab's interim door "Replace Keys" — its explanation lost the false truncate clause; batch 3 re-shapes the door whole to copy §4); the recovery words at Replace All Keys: the runner takes `recoveryWords: nil` (no recovery wrap — `beginOverAVaultWithARecoveryWrapLeavesNoStaleWrap` pins that NO stale wrap survives) or the words in hand — the door's checkboxes decide; the door authorizes by the ACCOUNT PASSWORD per the design (§4) — NOT built here (the interim door still takes the passphrase; the runner needs the passphrase regardless for the wraps — question 1's shape).
- **To the sitting (her hands, the real store):** the real-store switch timing — the drill's 4.1 s for 7,656 rows against an IN-MEMORY mirror is a floor; the real number is the network's (39 round trips per 7.6k rows at batch 200) — measure at the window; the migration drill on a byte-copy of her store and the 13l restore drill per posture are the sitting's (need her passphrase); the DEV store's own switch in the Debug app (the ceremony's face in her hands) — the doors render under Standard once a runner is wired at ready (they are); a marker-8 build is needed on both devices before any real round (the key proof lockstep — 1b).
- **To the ladder's reviewers:** the two UNPINNED resume arms (PINS — `restartRequired`; the follow-by-passphrase at `.reencrypt`); the ceremony's option (A) words road (question 1); the step-1 single-push backlog refusal (question 2).

#### MACHINERY NOTES
- The batch's DerivedData: `App/Variorum/DerivedData/pre13x-b0` (reused); logs beside it as `2b-*.log` (gitignored).

- **zsh does NOT word-split an unquoted `$VAR`:** `for t in $TYPES` handed all seventeen names to ONE `-only-testing:` argument — eleven suites ran and the rest silently matched nothing (the `Executed 0 tests` class) — `${(f)TYPES}` splits on newlines; every gate command here uses it. The 2b.3 record's 77/17 is the corrected re-run (`2b3-mac3.log`).
- **`-only-testing` names are TYPE names; a suite's DISPLAY name (`@Suite("…")`) is what the log prints** — the 2b.4 run "118 tests in 15 suites" for 16 requested types was `DataSafetyTests` (a FILE, holding `BackupRetentionTests` · `BackupRunTests` · `DataSafetyRunbookTests`); the gate run's list was built from the files' `struct`/`final class` declarations instead.
- **Two 14c-contract pins rewritten at 2b.3** (`EncryptionFlowTests`): `theRekeyDoorLandsInTheMandatoryPhraseStepWithNewCustody`'s runner now saves fresh custody itself (the truncate-and-heal contract is gone), and `theRekeyWiringRunsTheBrainAdoptsCustodyAndReEnqueues` became `theRekeyDoorStaysDormantUntilTheRootWiresTheRunnerAndTheMarkerReadIsWired` — the ROOT wires the runner, so the services-level pin can only assert dormancy + the marker read.
- **The emulated vault's rotation seq:** `VaultRPCEmulation` (byte-identical in both test targets — its 1b rule) mints `rotation_started_seq` from a private static counter that cannot know the mirror's sequence; `EmulatedVault` post-processes the RPC's success (a rotation that BEGAN — the previous key absent before, present after) by claiming the seq from the mirror (`claimSeq`, the fake server's `seq += 1`) and rewriting the row + the receipt — so "below the seq" reads exactly the rows pushed before the rotation, as the real RPC's `nextval` does. The emulation file itself is untouched.
- **`KeyVaultClient` holds its `load` seam WEAKLY** (the flow tests' `[weak self]` shape): a world that does not RETAIN the emulation object sees `URLError(.cancelled)` at the first vault read — the drill's first run (`2b6-drill.log`); the pins' `World` and the drill's `DrillWorld` retain it.
- **The seeder in a test context needs `uuid` / `date` / `withRandomNumberGenerator` overridden** (swift-dependencies records an "unimplemented" issue per bare read under the test harness) — the drill sets live generators; `withSampleWorld` sets deterministic ones.
- **The real `.passphrase` custody keeps the DEVICE IDENTITY in the plain sync item** (`SyncCustodyRecord(deviceID:, material: nil)`) — 1a's design read from the other side: the keys leave the item, the identity stays; a pin asserting the item GONE is wrong.
- **A fresh device's own three seeded `setting` rows arrive as MERGES** (locally dirty from the seed), so a "applied == rows pushed" count reads 3 short; `applied + merged` is the honest total.
- **`Outcome.reencrypted` counts THIS run's rows** — across a crash + resume the mirror's recorded batches carry the whole count; the drill's seams assert the batches.
- **The pane reads the pending marker on the sheet's close** (`.task(id: controller.activeSwitch?.id)`), never at autosave cadence; the runner's `pendingMarker()` is one `sync_state` read.

#### QUESTIONS FOR THE AUTHOR / ARCHITECT
1. **A DESIGN FORK, found reading before building — the Touch-ID-only arm of the two rotation doors cannot produce the new keys' wraps.** Entering Castle (§4 step 2) and Replace All Keys (§5) rewrite the vault with **M′ under P and under W**, and the new key file with **L′ under P and W** — every one of those wraps needs the SECRET-DERIVED key (`PassphraseKDF.deriveKey(P, descriptor)` / `RecoveryPhrase.recoveryWrapKey(W)`), and nothing in custody yields it: the vault holds M under K_P (not K_P), the key file holds L under K_P (not K_P), and decision 3.1 says P and W are NEVER stored. So "Touch ID, or the passphrase — confirms" (copy §2b Screen 4; §4; §5's Castle row for Replace All Keys) and "You already have twelve words. They still open your library." (Screen 3) cannot both hold: with Touch ID alone, M′ cannot be wrapped under P; with the words untyped, M′ cannot be wrapped under W (the vault would lose its recovery wrap — exactly the 14c `AccountRekey` shape the design retires). **Two honest options:** (A) **the ceremony types the passphrase ALWAYS** (Screen 4's confirmation is the passphrase, vault-checked — Touch ID is not sufficient because the new keys must be wrapped under it), and when twelve words EXIST the ceremony reuses 2a's Screen 3 ("Type My Words…" → the same twelve wrap M′ / "Generate New Ones" → fresh words replace them everywhere) instead of "They still open your library" — decision 5's letter ("Turning on sync requires a passphrase AND the twelve words…; so does entering Castle"), §7's follow (which writes only the TYPED secret's copy — the design plainly assumes no derived key is cached), zero new custody; or (B) **the per-store custody item gains the DERIVED wrapping keys** (K_P + its descriptor, K_W) beside M — no new power (M already equals both), validated against the vault's descriptor at use (a change elsewhere ⇒ fall to typing), refreshed whenever a word is typed — which makes Touch ID sufficient but contradicts §7's letter and widens what custody holds. **This batch BUILDS the runner over INPUT secrets** (a `RotationSecrets` value: the passphrase-derived key + its fresh descriptor, and the recovery-derived key or nil), so either answer plugs in without touching the driver, **and builds the ceremony on (A)'s road** (the typed passphrase, vault-checked; the words typed-or-regenerated when they exist) as the PROVISIONAL — the Touch-ID-only confirmation is NOT built. The architect rules before the copy is re-voiced; the recorded consequence under (A): a Castle entry without the words typed regenerates them (the standing CF-5 mandatory step), never silently drops the vault's recovery wrap.
2. **Step 1 pushes ONCE** (`engine.push()` — `maxPushIterations` × `batchSize` = 1,000 rows) and then refuses a non-empty outbox with the count (`SyncError.outboxNotEmpty`, worded). The scheduler's steady state makes that the "what this device owes" of the design; a device with a LARGER backlog (offline for days, then Use Castle…) meets the refusal and must let sync catch up first. Should the runner DRAIN instead (loop `push()` to empty, the drill's own setup) — a one-line change in `run` — or is the refusal the intended honesty (the design's letter: "refuse a non-empty outbox")? Built to the letter; the architect's call.
3. **Entering Castle lands Touch ID when biometry exists and Always ask otherwise** — the ceremony has no gate choice of its own (copy §2b has none); the pane's *Always ask* (Castle-only, `setAlwaysAsk`) is the switch after. Confirm that the first Castle open is Touch ID by default on a Mac with biometry, or should the floor screen offer the choice?
4. **The "still finishing" sentence's `[device]` is "another device"**: the vault carries no name for the device that began a rotation (the format has no such column, and adding one is a lockstep column — 13y's window or a later event). The day it started rides the vault's `updated_at`. Accept the anonymous device for v1, or record the acting device's name in the vault at the next lockstep event?
5. **Leaving Castle may confirm by Touch ID (`confirmPresence`) while entering must take the passphrase** — an asymmetry the wraps force (M′/L′ under P need P in hand; question 1's fork), not a design choice. Stated so the cold reviewer does not read it as an oversight.
6. **The resume's two unpinned arms** (PINS): `restartRequired` (the vault write never landed) and the follow-by-passphrase (the write landed, custody did not). Both are straight-line code over pinned primitives; a seam between the vault write and the custody save would pin them — worth a fault-injection leaf on `Leaves` at the ladder if the reviewer wants it red-on-old.

#### THE SETTLED DESIGN, PER ITEM
*(the LIVE STATUS lines above carry the built shape per item with their pins; this section holds what a reader needs beyond them)*
- **2c.0 the iOS keychain fence (corner 47):** the session token and the legacy 14b legs are scoped by STORE VARIANT — a dev store never reads the real store's session or its legacy custody items (`KeychainAuthStorage.inheritsUnscopedItems` is true for the real scope only; `LegacyLegs.forScope` hands the dev scope inert legs). The real scope's one-time inheritance of the unscoped 1a and 9c items stays (the migration road), pinned in `SessionTokenBindingTests` under throwaway services.
- **2c.0b decision 34:** `key_vault.updated_by_device` (the seventh column, nullable) is stamped by EVERY vault write with the writer's own OS name (`DeviceName.current` → `KeyVaultClient.Configuration.deviceName`, REQUIRED — a client without a name does not compile); the follow's notices read it back as *[device] on [day]* with `updated_at`; a NULL from a pre-2c writer reads *another device*. The deploy checklist says seven columns.
- **2c.1a / 2c.1b / 2c.1r:** the drain bound (three rounds, then the honest count); the two resume arms through a fault leaf; the runner's `follow` / `followIntoStandard` / (2c.1) `pickUpKeys` — as recorded in their LIVE STATUS lines.
- **2c.1 THE FOLLOW, the verdict's shape (one rule, both platforms, in `ProtectionController.reconcileFollow`):** with the vault's word in hand — (1) a DIFFERENT stamped owner ⇒ copy §3, no follow; (2) the same owner and material in hand: key ids differ ⇒ the follow (`.movedToCastle` iff the vault lost its Apple copy while the key file still has one, else `.keysReplaced`); key ids equal + the vault Apple-shaped + the key file Castle-shaped ⇒ the SILENT follow into Standard; (3) no material in hand (Always ask, decision 10's exception): the lobby-held derived key opens the vault's master when the descriptors agree ⇒ the session fills silently; else the key file's recorded corpus id ≠ the vault's ⇒ the follow, = ⇒ the Always-ask banner. The un-followed state is `followPending`: every key door speaks the one sentence; sync's refusals (the fence's AND the proof's) present as the replaced-keys row whose door reaches the Encryption tab, where the ONE door is the follow. *Later* is per launch (the persistent banner meanwhile; a new rotation re-asks).
- **2c.2 THE PHONE:** the SAME runner, models and words; the phone's faces only (the chooser doors, `MobilePostureSwitchSheet`, `MobileFollowSheet`, the notice bar's five arms). Castle on the phone = Face ID + the passphrase fallback + Always ask; the §11 migration is the shared one. Quick capture under a locked library refuses in words and keeps the text (§14.16 — `MobileQuickNoteUnderLockTests`, green on the simulator).

- Decision 34 (the author's): the seventh vault column carries the device's NAME, sent by every vault write; every notice renders *[device] on [day]* from it.

#### DRILLS RUN
**U3's four drills, run 2026-09-05 as ONE opt-in test over the whole sample library (`PostureSwitchDrillTests.standardToCastleWithASiblingThenBackToStandard`, `TEST_RUNNER_VARIORUM_POSTURE_DRILL=1`; a scratch keyed store — the dev-store class, never her store — with REAL Keychain items under throwaway scopes `Variorum-drill-…` / `…-sib`, against the emulated mirror + vault, this session's `2c-u3-drills.log`; `Test run with 2 tests in 1 suite passed after 76.818 seconds`).** The 2b drill was EXTENDED, not replaced: the drill's Standard vault now carries the Apple-held copy of M at setup (the 2a steady state — the same `settingAppleHeldCopy` + proof-carrying update leaving Castle performs; without it the vault was 14b-shaped and drill 1 could not exist — the first run failed `appleHeldCopyAbsent` and said so), and a SIBLING DEVICE (`makeSibling(of:)`) joins: its own scratch store pulled to head under the old keys, its own Standard-shaped key file, its own throwaway-scope custody, its own controller and runner — the same account, vault, mirror and Apple key. Quoted, in order:
- `seeded the sample library in 4.17 s — 2533 live takes; pushed 7656 rows under the old corpus key in 2.07 s`
- **Drill 1 — the Standard new-device road (nothing typed):** `U3 DRILL 1 — STANDARD NEW DEVICE, nothing typed: the Apple key opened the vault's master copy; pulled applied 7653, merged 3 of 7656` (the A half: `unwrapMaster(withAppleKey:)` → `materialFollowing` → a fresh store pulls every row; the enroll half — the device's own key file and custody — is batch 3's, as the brief says).
- `SIBLING (Standard, old keys) pulled to head in 4.41 s: applied 7653, merged 3`
- **Drill 3 — Standard → Castle with the sibling following:** `STANDARD → CASTLE in 5.81 s — 7656 rows re-encrypted; insurance off: true; pre-switch pairs: ["variorum-backup-…-ce80be8c.sqlite"]`; then **an old snapshot cannot enroll:** `U3 — an old snapshot cannot enroll: the Apple road is closed, the old master fails the proof, the old pair opens only its own bytes` (asserted: no Apple-held copy in the vault; `KeyProof.hash(master: old M) != keyProofHash`; `unwrapMaster(withAppleKey:)` throws; the pre-switch pair's key file still opens under the OLD M — never rewritten); the sibling's old-key pull refused `SyncError.encryptionKeysReplaced` at the fence; a wrong word on the sibling refused `wrongPassphrase` with custody unchanged; then through the REAL runner: `follow: M′ picked up from the vault by the passphrase — no rows remain` · `re-keyed the library file in 0.070 s (under L′)` · `follow: done — Castle; library key 8f682c41` · `U3 DRILL 3 — the SIBLING FOLLOWED (passphrase once) in 1.82 s: re-encrypted 0 rows (the acting device had finished the walk); rekeys 1; insurance off: false; offer: 0 pairs` (asserted: the sibling's silent store-key item gone, its plain item keeps ONLY the identity, its session holds M′/C′, its new key file carries no Apple copy and opens under the passphrase, its old library key opens nothing, no marker); `the followed sibling's next pull: applied 7656, merged 0, cursor 15313` — every take intact (2533 of 2533).
- **Drill 2 — the Castle new-device road (passphrase once):** a fresh device `VaultEnrollment.enroll(passphrase:)` → `rotation == nil` → pulls every row under C′ (`applied + merged == 7656`; a sampled 200+-char take reads back byte-for-byte).
- **Drill 4 — Castle → Standard:** `leaving Castle: the vault gained the Apple-held copy under 52a2025d` · `CASTLE → STANDARD in 0.018 s` · `U3 DRILL 4 — CASTLE → STANDARD: the Apple-held copies are back, custody silent, nothing rotated` (asserted: the same corpus key id, the mirror untouched, the silent item holds L′, the plain sync record back); then the sibling's SILENT follow into Standard through the runner: `follow: the account is back in Standard — the Apple copy landed, custody silent` · `the sibling followed into Standard in 0.007 s` (its key file carries this account's A; its silent item and plain record back).
- The 2b seam drill (`aCrashAtEachSeamResumesToCompletionOverTheSampleLibrary`) ran green in the same run — SEAM A resumed in 2.32 s, B in 0.97 s (no passphrase needed — the master copy), C in 0.92 s.
**Mechanism note from the drill (for the reviewer, not a defect):** a follower whose rotation is already finished re-encrypts nothing and does NOT pull inside `follow` — `reencryptAndComplete` pulls windows only while a rotation is in flight; the resumed sync's first ordinary round then applies the re-stamped rows (the walk re-stamps every row at a fresh `server_seq`, so each follower re-applies the corpus ONCE per rotation — `applied 7656` above; cost ∝ the corpus, once, per follower — the design's in-place re-encryption consequence).

The pin now substitutes word by word until the checksum actually breaks (12 misses in a row: 16^-12). Recorded rather than filed: the fix is inside this batch's commit and the pin's meaning is unchanged.
- **The parity C5 sweep caught the 2c.0b fallback:** `MobileStructuralTests.nothingUserFacingNamesTheMac` flagged `DeviceName.swift: "this Mac"` (the macOS arm's fallback literal — inside `#if os(macOS)`, but the sweep is textual over `Shared/`, and rightly so); fixed by routing both arms' fallback through `ProtectionCopy.deviceName` (*this Mac* / *this iPhone* from the one word source). The first full phone run was 610 / 98 with that one red; the re-run at HEAD is the SUITE COUNTS line.
- The drill's first run failed `appleHeldCopyAbsent`: the 2b drill's Standard vault was 14b-shaped (P only — `setUp` writes no Apple copy; the app's 2a reconcile does, through a hook the drill never wired), so drill 1 (the Standard new-device road) could not exist on it. The drill now writes the Apple-held copy at setup through the same act leaving Castle performs. A reviewer reading the 2b drill record should know its "Standard" start state was Standard by the KEY FILE only.

#### QUESTIONS FOR THE AUTHOR / ARCHITECT
1. **The follow's `Later` cadence — a reading, not a silence:** the design says the notice *returns at every open*; built as per LAUNCH (deferred once per process; the persistent banner meanwhile; a NEW rotation re-asks at once). If "every open" means every unlock under Castle (a relock + unlock within one launch), `followDeferredThisLaunch` should reset on the lock road — one line, the architect's call.
2. **Replace All Keys at the Encryption tab still takes the passphrase alone** (`rekeyRunner` → `runner.replaceAllKeys(passphrase:recoveryWords: nil)`): decision 33 says both rotation doors take P typed + W typed or regenerated. 2b's record handed the door to batch 3; confirming it stays there (the runner's entry already accepts `recoveryWords`).
3. **The Always-ask retired-word banner fires only when the lobby-held derived key cannot open the vault** — so a passphrase change elsewhere is detected on an Always-ask device at its next open, never on a Touch ID / Face ID device (nothing there compares descriptors until a rotation changes the key id). The design's §7 post-unlock prompt for those devices is batch 4's; recorded so the reviewer does not read the gap as a missed mechanism.
4. **The drill's sibling followed with `insurance off: false`** — the sibling world never had the plaintext insurance on, so nothing flipped; the acting device's flip is asserted. If the reviewer wants the follower's flip drilled too, one line arms it (`MarkdownInsurance.setEnabled(true, in:)` on the sibling's defaults before the follow).

1. **`Later`'s cadence (question 1) — RULED (architect):** "every open" means every OPEN of the library — under Castle a relock + unlock is an open, so `followDeferredThisLaunch` resets on the lock road (one line). Batch 4 lands it with the follow notices' remainder.

#### THE SETTLED DESIGN, PER ITEM (the readings the brief left to the implementer — written BEFORE the code, revised in place as each lands)
1. **Decision 35 — the derivation.** `Posture.derive` returns a `PostureReading` — `.determined(Posture)` or `.indeterminate(shape: Posture)` — and takes a REQUIRED `enrolled: Bool` (the `legacyCleanup` precedent: every call site decides). The precedence, as the brief states it: (1) the vault's word in hand ⇒ determined (an Apple-held copy ⇒ Standard, none ⇒ Castle) — a vault word means the account exists, so it outranks the legacy mode too; (2) `enrolled` with no vault word ⇒ INDETERMINATE, carrying the posture the CUSTODY SHAPE reads as (silent ⇒ Standard, gated-or-nowhere ⇒ Castle, unknown ⇒ the key file's 2a rule) — what the routes and the gate act on ("opens through the custody it HAS"); (3) not enrolled ⇒ the legacy mode; (4) else the key file (2a's rule (c) with corner 45), the safety arm last. `StoreAtOpenMode.effective` acts on the reading's ACTING posture. **What "enrolled" means where:** at the ROUTE (no session object, no network) — the per-store sync custody's PRESENCE (the silent item readable or the gated item's non-prompting probe — a new `StoreLaunchGate.syncCustodyPresent` seam); in the CONTROLLER and the MIGRATION — signed in (a new `isSignedIn` hook from `auth.currentUserID`, wired in `wireOneSecretHooks`) OR sync custody present (the session's record, the silent item, or the gated probe). Under INDETERMINATE nothing posture-driven writes: the reconcile's Apple arm returns before reading or minting A and leaves `appleKeyVerified` untouched; the route's 2b-item-(0) Apple landing is skipped for an enrolled library; the exposed nudge needs a DETERMINED Standard; the migration runs legs 1–2 (the custody RE-SCOPE under the shape it has — the credential audit unchanged — and the 13p escrow removal), then stops before leg 3 with one line and WITHOUT marking done, so legs 3–6 (the dial-key retirement, the alignment, the key file, the confirm) run at the next ready with the vault's word. **The next ready exists now:** `reconcile()` had exactly two production callers — the bootstrap's ready road (which runs BEFORE the root's `.task` wires the vault hook and restores the session — the window's "vault descriptor: next online ready" that never came) and the runner's leaves. The bootstrap gains `runReadyHousekeeping()` (the migration-if-needed + the reconcile, ONE act, its task awaitable), the ready road calls it, and BOTH roots call it again after `wireOneSecretHooks` + `services.start()` — the vault readable, the session restored — which is what "retries at the next ready" means in-process.
2. **The posture-mismatch follow.** `reconcileFollow`, with the vault's word and custody's material in hand and the corpus key ids EQUAL (no rotation): the library key's custody SHAPE (the resident key after adoption ⇒ gated-or-nowhere; no resident ⇒ silent; before adoption ⇒ unknown, no verdict) against the account's posture — Castle in the vault + the silent shape ⇒ `followNotice = .movedToCastle` (copy §5 verbatim, *[device] on [day]* from `updated_by_device` / `updated_at`, "another device" when NULL; *Later* the same as 2c's); Standard in the vault + a gated-or-nowhere shape ⇒ the silent `followIntoStandardAct` (2c's key-file trigger kept; the shape trigger added). Never while a switch is in progress (the re-key's reopen re-runs the reconcile mid-road). **The runner's NO-ROTATION road** in `follow(passphrase:recoveryWords:)`: the typed secret is verified against the vault's copy exactly as before (`VaultEnrollment.enroll` — a wrong word refuses typed, nothing written); when custody's corpus key id equals the vault's and no previous key is carried, the new-master half (`materialFollowing`, the `.reencrypt` marker, the custody replacement, the walk) is SKIPPED — custody already holds M and C — and the road goes straight to 2b's steps 4–6 through `rekeyCustodyAndSnapshot` with the HELD material: a new L′ (a fresh file by export-and-swap), a NEW key file under the new fingerprint (L′ under P with the VAULT's descriptor + under M; the W copy when the words were typed; NO `apple` copy; the vault's descriptor and corpus key id recorded), custody → the gate (`moveCustody`, both items), the fresh pair, the insurance off, sync resumed, the reconcile. Then, on EVERY follow into Castle (the no-rotation road and 2c's rotation road alike), the OLD key file is REMOVED from the live folder (a Standard-era key file beside a Castle library is the exposure itself; the snapshot pairs keep their own; `locate` then finds exactly one file). Not on the ceremony's own road (2b's letter: the old file stays there) and not on a marker RESUME of a crashed follow (the marker does not know it was a follow) — recorded under QUESTIONS.
3. **The confirm gate.** The one-time confirm (copy §9) is owed when the persisted mark says so (the migration's leg 6 — now set for EVERY enrolled library that reached leg 5, the vault reachable or not, since the confirm's act — re-wrap the local copy under the vault's descriptor and record it — is owed either way) OR by DERIVATION for the already-migrated class: an enrolled library whose key file carries the MASTER copy (leg 5's signature — the migration ran) but NO recorded descriptor (`vaultKDF == nil` — the vault was unreachable at that instant: her Mac). Typing it lands `vaultKDF` + the reconciled copy through 2a's `reconcilePassphraseWrap`, which closes the derived condition by itself; *Later* hides the face for the launch and keeps the mark. The gate is deliberately NOT "every enrolled library with `vaultKDF == nil`": a library enrolled through the 14c-era Encryption tab carries a passphrase copy under a FRESH descriptor and no master copy, and copy §9's sentence (*Variorum now keeps your sync keys…*) is her migration's, not a fresh enrollee's. The migration's leg-5 line says "at the next passphrase entry" — and, by construction after item 1, a fresh migration of an enrolled library only reaches leg 5 with the vault's word in hand, so the line now speaks only for the already-migrated class.

#### ORCHESTRATOR-DECIDED NOTES (settled under the mandate without the author — for the cold reviewer)
- The precedence rule as the orchestrator read decision 35 (the brief's item 1): enrolled-or-signed-in ⇒ the vault's word, else INDETERMINATE — the legacy stored mode never overrides an enrolled library (it is a fact about the gate the device had, honored by opening through the EXISTING custody shape); not enrolled ⇒ the legacy mode, else the key file, else the safety arm only with no session AND no custody.
- The no-rotation follow road (the brief's item 2): skip the new-master half; the secret verified against the vault's copy; `moveCustody` both items; the library RE-KEYED via 2b's `rekeyLibrary`; a NEW key file with NO `apple` copy; the OLD key file REMOVED from the live folder; re-lock on.
- The confirm gate on `vaultKDF == nil` for an enrolled library, not on the passphrase copy's absence; the migration's line "at the next passphrase entry".

#### THE SETTLED DESIGN, PER ITEM (the readings the brief left to the implementer — written BEFORE the code, revised in place as each lands)
0. **The identity carry.** Leg 1's material-less arm writes the identity-only record itself (the brief's "one road"): `SyncCustodyMigration.migrateSyncCustodyToStoreScope` stays the MATERIAL road (its `guard let material` unchanged), and leg 1 handles the row-only case beside it — the `CustodyHandover` discipline in miniature (write, verify by the production read, only then delete the row). Decision 13's letter is precised for the migration class exactly as the brief states: the pre-13x library's identity lives in `sync_state`, and leg 1 is where that source is read.
1. **Sync setup.** ONE core road: `VaultEnrollment.setUp(passphrase:recoveryWords:appleHeldCopy:kdf:)` — `recoveryWords` REQUIRED (the twelve words in hand: generated + confirmed, or typed and verified against the key file); `appleHeldCopy` = `(key, id)` under Standard, nil under Castle; `kdf` defaults to a fresh v1 descriptor, and the APP passes the key file's current passphrase descriptor so the vault's M-wrap and the file's L-wrap share one derivation (§3.6 — "derive ONCE when the recorded descriptor equals the vault's"; recorded as a reading — the brief's signature is `setUp(passphrase:recoveryWords:appleHeldCopy:)`). The old `setUp(passphrase:)` stays in core for the headless `vault-bootstrap` tool and the core pins; `EncryptionSession.setUp(passphrase:)` RETIRES (the app's door takes the words — a test-target convenience generates them for the 14b-era pins). The posture Screen 1 speaks from is the LIBRARY's OWN (`ProtectionController.libraryOwnPosture` — the key-file rule with `enrolled: false`; corner 26: the first device gives the vault its shape); the Apple copy is read-or-minted under Standard through `StoreTie.appleKey`. The ceremony brain `SyncSetupModel` (Shared): `.confirmMove` (the foreign-owner confirm, only when `foreignOwnerExists`) → `.explain` → `.passphrase` (set when none: ≥ 12 + matched + `controller.setPassphrase`; TYPE when the library has one: `verifyPassphraseAgainstStore`, wrong ⇒ `passphraseNotTheLibrarys`) → the words through `WordsCeremony` (existing: Type My Words… verified against the key file / Generate New Ones — the file's copy REPLACED at the land; none: generate → written down → confirmed by exact re-entry) → `.creating` → `.done`. After the create: custody (`store.save`), the landing hook (the master copy + `recordVaultReconciliation(kdf: the vault's, corpusKeyID:)`; the W copy landed on the store when the words were typed or generated here), the adopt road when the confirm was taken. The 14b stepped setup (`setupSection`, the CF-7 checkbox, the mandatory-phrase step AS SETUP's step 4) retires; `mandatoryPhraseSection` stays for a 14b-era vault that still has no recovery wrap (the `enrolled(hasRecoveryWrap: false)` state exists on the wire).
2. **Enroll.** `EnrollFacts { accountPosture (from the fetched vault); libraryPosture (the library's own); appleState (matched · absent · differentAppleID — the reconcile's read of A against the vault's `apple_key_id`) }` projected on `EncryptionSession` from `refresh()` + the `StoreTie`; the `.notEnrolled` arm renders per direction: Standard/Standard ⇒ the waiting face until (a) lands at the next ready (the reconcile runs it; the tab's *Enter Passphrase…* / *Enter Twelve Words…* stand beside it); Castle/Castle ⇒ copy §2d's title + the roads; Castle account + Standard-born library ⇒ the title + the sentence, the act = the follow (kind `.standardBornJoinsCastle` — the runner's road with no custody held: M from the vault, the `.reencrypt` marker, custody saved, `complete` (a no-op write when no previous key), the re-key, custody behind the gate, the offer when the key file carried an Apple copy, the old key file retired); Standard account + Castle-born library ⇒ the consent sentence + *Join* / *Cancel*, Join = A opens M (the roads when A cannot) → custody under the CURRENT shape → the master copy → `runner.followIntoStandard()`. (a)'s custody landing rides the session's own save (`landEnrollment`, wired by the roots to `EncryptionSession.adoptEnrolledMaterial`) so the reconcile never grows a second custody writer.
3. **The adopt road.** `Queries.foreignOwnerExists(_:signedIn:)` — `SELECT 1 … WHERE owner_id IS NOT NULL AND owner_id != ? LIMIT 1` per owner-carrying synced table; the verdict's read (`ProtectionController.foreignOwner`, wired by the bootstrap over the store). `Operations.restampOwner(to:)`: `UPDATE t SET owner_id = ? WHERE owner_id IS NOT ?` for every synced table carrying the column (PRAGMA-introspected like `adoptOwnerID`), tombstones included; NOT apply mode (the triggers enqueue; `resyncReinitialize` enqueues every row anyway); refuses signed out. `AppServices.connectLibrary(passphrase:recoveryWords:)`: the sync chain stopped → `VaultEnrollment.enroll` (a wrong word refuses before anything is written) → the restamp → `SyncEngine(transport: makeSyncTransport(auth), keyProvider: makeSyncKeyProvider()).resyncReinitialize()` under the session's database → custody through the session's adopt (+ the landing hook) → `connectSyncIfAllowed()`. The bars' *Connect…* and the Encryption tab's door open `PostureFollowModel` with `FollowNotice(kind: .connectLibrary)` — the existing Mac `FollowSheet` / phone `MobileFollowSheet` render it (the notice body = copy §3's sentence; the doors *Enter Passphrase…* · *Use Twelve Words…*; the third door reads *Cancel* for this kind and closes without deferring; Done renders `followDoneBody`, the copy file having no Connect line — a reading). The setup ceremony's adopt = `AppServices.adoptLibraryAfterSetup()` (the restamp + resync with the material just minted).
4. **The gate.** Per the brief's item 4; the drills as one opt-in suite over the sample library (`ConnectDrillTests`, `TEST_RUNNER_VARIORUM_CONNECT_DRILL=1`) beside the existing `PostureSwitchDrillTests` — the Standard enroll half + the Castle new-device road under throwaway scopes, the adopt road against a per-account emulated mirror.

#### ORCHESTRATOR-DECIDED NOTES (settled under the mandate without the author — for the cold reviewer)
- The leg-1 identity carry (ledger 43): leg 1 carries `sync_state.device_id` into an identity-only per-store record whether or not legacy MATERIAL exists — the orchestrator's precision of §11's letter within the mandate (decision 13's premise is false for exactly the migration class).
- The exact foreign-owner read: a library is FOREIGN when ANY owner-carrying synced row's `owner_id` is non-NULL and ≠ the signed-in id (a mixed library reads foreign) — replacing `stampedOwnerID`'s first-row read in the verdict.
- The enroll-is-a-follow reading: a Standard-born library joining a Castle account IS a follow into Castle with the secret just typed — `PostureSwitchRunner.follow` is the road, never a copy.
- The PROVISIONAL consent string for a Castle-born library joining a Standard account (ledger 46 — her words pending): *"This account is in Standard, so this library will open silently from now on and your Apple account will hold a key to it. To keep only-you keys for the whole account, enter Castle instead — Settings ▸ Protection ▸ Use Castle…"*, doors *Join* / *Cancel*.
- Two settled facts binding both sessions: (i) `key_vault_update` keeps a landed proof hash when a payload omits one; (ii) the runner's `recoveryWords: nil` guard is 3b's item 3.

The `landRecoveryWrap(allowReplace:)` add arm stays for the regenerate door.

#### MACHINERY NOTES
- **`-only-testing` takes TYPE names, and a name that matches nothing is SILENTLY dropped** — twice this batch: `Phase8ServicesTests` (a FILE, not a type) and a retyped phone list with six phantoms that "passed" 7 tests in 2 suites. The audit line `Test run with N tests in M suites` is the only honest count — read it every run; `OwnerIdentityTests` is a CORE suite (it ran under `swift test`, never under xcodebuild). The phone's real eight: `MobileFollowDetectionTests MobileOneSecretHooksTests MobilePhoneLobbyTests MobilePhoneProtectionCopyTests MobilePhoneStoreEncryptionTests MobileProtectionSurfaceTests MobileStructuralTests MobileWindowReplayTests`.
- zsh expands a leading `=word` (`echo ====X` → "not found"); `#expect`'s autoclosure takes no `await`/`try` (bind locals first — three times this batch); a MainActor-isolated static referenced from a Sendable closure must be `nonisolated`; `LockIsolated` needs `import Dependencies`.
- The 2c Always-ask pin's bare envelopes read as NEVER ENROLLED under 3a.2's discriminator (no master copy && no recorded corpus id) — its worlds gained the enrollment signature (the master copy + the recorded id); recorded as a reading, not a behaviour change.
- `KeyVaultClient.load` holds its responder WEAKLY — every scripted vault must be retained by the test (the 2b drill's `vaultEmu` note holds here too).
- The batch's DerivedData: `App/Variorum/DerivedData/pre13x-b0` (the Mac, reused), `pre13x-b0-ios` (the simulator tests), `pre13x-b0-ios-build` (the generic iOS build); logs beside them as `3a-*.log` (gitignored); pre-revert copies in `3a-prerevert/` (gitignored, `git check-ignore` confirmed).

#### QUESTIONS FOR THE AUTHOR / ARCHITECT
*(none gated the build; every one is a READING the implementer settled under the mandate and records for the orchestrator's adjudication — the design was silent or ambiguous exactly here)*
1. **The setup's kdf is SHARED between the key file and the vault** — `EncryptionSession.setUp` passes the key file's current passphrase descriptor (`StoreTie.passphraseKDF`) as the vault's `kdf`, so the vault's M-wrap and the file's L-wrap share one derivation from birth (§3.6's "derive ONCE when the recorded descriptor equals the vault's"). The brief's signature was `setUp(passphrase:recoveryWords:appleHeldCopy:)`; the `kdf` parameter (defaulting fresh) is the implementer's addition. Confirm, or rule the vault derives its own.
2. **The never-enrolled discriminator** in `reconcileFollow`: a library is NEVER ENROLLED here iff the key file carries NO master copy AND NO recorded corpus key id (and no sync custody item was present at ready). Any master copy or recorded id means "enrolled once" and the follow's ordinary verdicts apply. This re-based one 2c pin (the Always-ask worlds). Confirm.
3. **A fresh silent library joining a CASTLE account takes direction (c) — the follow with the typed secret** (ONE passphrase entry; the library re-keys as it joins — drill (ii)); the design's "enroll by the passphrase" for a Castle account is read as this follow, never a bare custody save beside a Standard-shaped key file. Confirm.
4. **The store-less composition rule at setup** (3a.1): with NO library posture in view (no store tie wired — the tool-class composition) the vault is born WITHOUT the Apple copy; a STANDARD library whose A the Keychain will not hold refuses the setup WHOLE (`appleKeyRefusedAtSetup`) rather than minting a Castle-shaped vault the user never chose. Confirm the refusal (the alternative — a vault born Castle-shaped with a sentence — was rejected as a posture decided by a Keychain failure).
5. **`restampOwner(to:)` takes the owner EXPLICITLY** (the services pass the signed-in id) and rewrites every owner-carrying row whose `owner_id` is NULL or ≠ that id, tombstones included, in ONE transaction with the outbox triggers LIVE (not apply mode — `resyncReinitialize` re-enqueues every row anyway). Confirm the NULL-owner rows joining the re-stamp.
6. **The (a) silent enroll is REACHABLE only through `landEnrollment`** — wired by `wireOneSecretHooks` at both roots to `EncryptionSession.adoptEnrolledMaterial`; a composition that never wires it leaves the face owed instead (the pins' bare controllers). Deliberate — the session owns custody. Confirm.
7. **Connect's DONE line reuses `followDoneBody`** (copy §3 has no done sentence for Connect) — batch 4's to word.
8. **The PROVISIONAL consent string** for a Castle-born library joining a Standard account (ledger 46) — rendered verbatim as recorded under ORCHESTRATOR-DECIDED NOTES; her words pending.
9. **`hasRecoveryWrap: false` after a SUCCESSFUL `rekeyAccount`** stands in the 14b pins (742/764) — the rekey road does not yet carry W; 3b's item 3 per the settled facts. Confirm the hand-off rather than a 3a fix.

#### ORCHESTRATOR ADJUDICATION — batch 3a (verified against git before adoption: 12 implementer commits `4457fce8`…`86701c28` interleaved with the orchestrator's `9a5abe68` / `baf4948f` on the shared tree, every hash an ancestor of HEAD; HEAD == `origin/main`; tree clean; ZERO `Server/` and ZERO `Sync/` lines over the whole range (the re-stamp is `Operations/OwnerIdentity.swift`, the read `Queries/OwnerQueries.swift`); the four logs' exit lines read exactly as recorded — core 1442/157 · the gate 227/32 with no `skipped` line · the drills 3/2 · the phone 41/8 — and the ten Tutorial suites are present by name in the gate log; `ProtectionMigration` leg 1's material-less arm, `Queries.foreignOwnerExists`, `Operations.restampOwner`, `WordsCeremony`, `SyncSetupModel`/`View`, `EncryptionSession.storeTie` / `onVaultMaterialLanded` / `adoptEnrolledMaterial` and both roots' wiring read in the code; the record's counts adopted as recorded)
1. **Question 1 (the vault born under the key file's descriptor) — CONFIRMED (architect):** §3.6's derive-once rule applied at birth, exactly as the switch already writes L′ under the vault's fresh descriptor; one derivation opens both copies and the two can never disagree.
2. **Question 2 (the never-enrolled discriminator) — RULED WITH A CORRECTION → 3b's item 0 (ledger 52):** the "no master copy, no recorded id" gate excludes RESTORED and COPIED libraries, which §3.8, §6 and §7 say enroll — Standard with nothing typed, Castle with the word just typed to open. The silent Standard enroll's precondition is "no custody material in hand + a recorded id that is nil or the vault's"; a STALE recorded id owes the follow's notice for any device; the derived-key fill runs for any gate and lands custody under the current shape. The reading's re-base of the 2c Always-ask pin is undone with it where it no longer gates.
3. **Question 3 (a Standard-born library joining Castle = the follow with the typed secret) — CONFIRMED** (the brief's own reading; reuse, never a copy).
4. **Question 4 (the store-less composition rule; a refused iCloud Keychain refuses the setup whole) — CONFIRMED:** a posture decided by a Keychain failure is the one thing the derivation must never do; the refusal names the fix. Its sentence (`appleKeyRefusedAtSetup`) is PROVISIONAL — batch 4's copy list, 13s's voice.
5. **Question 5 (`restampOwner` re-stamps NULL-owner rows too) — CONFIRMED:** an unowned row is a row born signed-out on this library; it uploads under the account it joins, exactly as `adoptOwnerID` always claimed NULLs.
6. **Question 6 (the silent enroll reachable only through the wired `landEnrollment`) — CONFIRMED:** the session owns custody; the reconcile records, it never grows a second custody writer (2d's precedent).
7. **Question 7 (Connect's done line) — DRAFTED by the orchestrator into copy §3 the same day** (*This library is connected to this account, and everything in it is uploading now.*); batch 4 renders it (`connectDoneBody`); 13s re-voices.
8. **Question 8 (the provisional consent string) — RECORDED;** her re-wording rides 13s / batch 5 (ledger 46).
9. **Question 9 (`hasRecoveryWrap: false` after a successful rekey) — CONFIRMED as 3b's item 3** (the runner's nil-words guard + the door carrying W).
10. **A RECORDS FINDING, fixed by the orchestrator:** the record's revert quotes for batches B, C and D had been inserted at the file's FIRST `#### DRILLS RUN` heading — inside the 1a record's PINS (pre-edit lines 1269–1288) — instead of this record's; moved here after batch A. **The orchestrator's own first move (`9f3d2dd9`) mis-sliced the file** (it cut from those lines to the 2c record's batch B and carried 198 unrelated lines with them); the commit after it restored the file from `86701c28` and redid the move on the exact bounds — the diff against `86701c28` is the proof: only the three blocks moved, plus the edits this adjudication names.
11. **3a is ADOPTED; U4 continues at 3b** (a fresh Fable; the brief refined at this seam — the "REFINED AT 3a's SEAM" paragraph and item 0).
